10 月外贸数据点评：出口高位放缓，结构支撑仍
强

[Table_Author] 
 魏争 分析师  沈夏宜 分析师

Email:weizheng@lczq.com Email:shenxiayi@lczq.com  
证书:S1320524100001 证书:S1320523020004

### Page 2

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 2 
响，但较 2024 年同期的两年复合增速仍约为 29.3%，表明半导体产业链
需求确有较强支撑。10 月韩国半导体出口增长 259.4%1，也能印证全球
AI 及半导体景气仍处高位。二是，对欧洲及其他主要市场出口普遍增长。
10 月，对欧盟地区出口增长 24.3%，拉动出口 3.5 个百分点；对德国、
印度、俄罗斯和加拿大分别增长 33.5%、43.7%、31.9%和 31.6%。三
是，对美国出口继续形成正贡献，趋势性恢复仍需观察。10 月，对美出
口增长 32.9%，拉动整体出口约 3.8 个百分点，低基数仍是主要原因，去
年同期对美出口下降 25.2%，本月对美出口金额较 2024 年同期仍低约
0.5%，尚未明显突破前期水平。 
从量价看，出口结构较上半年更均衡。一方面，传统消费品及部分装备产
品继续获得数量支撑。在跟踪的 11 类商品中，成品油、箱包、鞋靴、钢
材、铝材、家用电器和汽车等 7 类呈现量价齐升。其中，箱包出口数量增
长 9.6%、均价上涨 7.5%；鞋靴数量增长 1.0%、均价上涨 13.1%；家用
电器和汽车数量分别增长 14.1%和 33.5%，均价分别上涨 5.9%和 3.1%。
另一方面，电子产品仍主要由价格拉动。集成电路出口数量下降 2.5%、
平均单价上涨 143.2%；手机数量下降 18.2%、平均单价上涨 38.4%，指
向电子产品出口金额对高单价品类的依赖提升。 
进口增速明显抬升，机电产品和能源价格仍是主要动力，实体数量需求整
体偏弱。10 月，进口同比增长 41.6%，较 9 月提高 10.6 个百分点；环比
下降 2.2%。去年同期进口仅增长 1.8%，对本月同比形成一定低基数支
撑，结构上看，进口高增主要源于机电产业链和能源价格。一是机电产品
贡献进一步扩大。10 月，机电产品进口增长 57.5%，拉动整体进口 23.5
个百分点。其中，自动数据处理设备增长 261.0%，拉动 8.0 个百分点；
集成电路增长 78.3%，拉动 13.7 个百分点，拆分量价看，进口数量基本
持平，平均单价上涨 78.3%。二是能源进口主要由价格上涨推动。原油、
成品油和天然气进口金额分别增长 41.0%、45.2%和 50.2%，合计拉动进
口约 5.9 个百分点；平均进口价格分别上涨 62.6%、61.7%和 46.7%，数
量分别下降 13.3%、下降 10.2%和增长 2.4%。三是金属资源品继续分
化。铜矿进口增长 23.1%，数量下降 11.6%，金额增长主要来自价格；铁
矿石进口下降 11.5%，数量和价格分别下降 5.1%和 6.7%；稀土进口增长
19.1%，主要由数量增长 18.3%支撑。四是农产品转为进口拖累。农产品
进口下降 2.2%，拖累整体进口约 0.2 个百分点；大豆进口下降 12.5%，
数量下降 22.5%，平均价格上涨 12.9%。 
往后看，出口短期仍有支撑，中长期动能持续性需要观察。一方面，短期
出口具备韧性条件。一是全球制造业和新出口订单仍处扩张区间，主要科
技企业也在推进数据中心、服务器和芯片投资，我们分析 AI 链条短期仍
会支撑出口；二是传统商品出口仍然存在低基数效应，后续拖累出口的概
率不大。第 140 届广交会现场意向出口成交额 267.5 亿美元，一定程度上
有助于订单拓展2。另一方面，全球货币环境趋紧或形成约束。中长期出
口高增的持续性仍需观察。三季度以来全球主要央行先后加息或维持高利

### Page 3

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 3 
率，全球货币环境重新趋紧。一方面，融资成本上升可能抑制主要经济体
的投资和制造业需求；另一方面，高利率会提高 AI 基础设施项目的资金
成本和投资回报要求。如果 AI 商业化收入兑现偏慢，相关企业可能调整
项目建设节奏，全球 AI 资本开支增速存在从高位放缓的风险，或会对我
国集成电路、自动数据处理设备及相关中间品等出口产生影响。

风险提示：全球制造业景气超预期回落；全球 AI 投资需求不及预期。

投资评级说明 
投资建议的评级标准  评级 说明 
评级标准为报告发布日后的 6 个月内公司股价（或行业
指数）相对同期基准指数的相对市场表现。其中 A 股市
场以沪深 300 指数为基准；新三板市场以三板成指（针
对协议转让标的）或三板做市指数（针对做市转让标的）
为基准；香港市场以恒生指数为基准。 
股票评级 
买入 相对同期基准指数涨幅在 10%以上 
增持 相对同期基准指数涨幅在 5%~10%之间 
中性 相对同期基准指数涨幅在-5%~+5%之间 
减持 相对同期基准指数跌幅在 5%以上 
行业评级 
看好 相对表现优于市场 
中性 相对表现与市场持平 
看淡 相对表现弱于市场

Document 041: 20261027_投資週報_AI資金壓力擾動市場，科技基本面仍穩健

金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 10 月 27 日
上週重點訊息
◼ AI 投資回報疑慮升溫，資金轉向債市避險：市場對大型科技企業 AI 基礎建設投資規模、融資
需求及未來獲利回收能力的疑慮升溫，導致科技股賣壓擴大，10 月 22 日 Nasdaq 指數下跌
約 2.6%。隨著市場風險偏好下降，避險資金轉向美國公債，10 年期公債殖利率單日下降約 19
個基點，創 2023 年 3 月以來最大單日跌幅，並於 23 日回升至 4.99%。市場關注焦點逐漸由
AI 投資規模轉向資本支出效益，後續大型科技企業財報及現金流表現，將成為評估 AI 產業評

價合理性的重要依據。
◼ 中東衝突推升通膨風險：中東衝突持續升溫，伊朗支持的葉門胡塞武裝攻擊沙烏地阿拉伯，引
發市場對原油供應中斷的擔憂，推升布蘭特原油價格於 10 月 23 日升至每桶 116.72 美元，單
週上漲約 6.2%，創 2022 年以來最高週收盤水準。能源價格攀升加劇全球通膨壓力，並壓抑
市場風險偏好。
◼ 法國政治動盪加劇財政疑慮，歐洲主權債風險重新受到關注：法國因生活成本上升爆發大規模
抗議，進一步加深市場對政府財政整頓能力的疑慮。10 月 19 日法國 10 年期公債殖利率上升
約 10 個基點至 4.88%附近，與德國公債利差擴大至約 144 個基點，創 2012 年以來最高收盤
水準，法股單日下跌約 1.5%，歐元同步走弱。市場擔憂政治不確定性可能削弱財政改革進度，
並透過主權債評價影響銀行業資產負債表。後續須關注法國財政政策、歐洲主權債利差及銀行
融資成本變化。
市場展望
◼ 科技股短線宜審慎，中期聚焦基本面穩健的 AI 企業：近期 AI 資本支出、融資需求及評價調整

使科技股波動加劇，但先進製程與半導體設備需求仍具韌性，AI 中長期成長趨勢未改變。隨市
場焦點由成長題材轉向投資效益，企業營收與訂單能見度、自由現金流及資本效率的重要性提
升，具技術優勢、財務穩健及獲利能見度的企業相對有利，而高度依賴外部融資者宜審慎看待。
◼ 美債避險功能重現，中天期債券風險報酬相對均衡：市場風險偏好降溫時，美國公債仍具一定
避險效果。儘管 Fed 貨幣政策路徑與能源價格增添利率不確定性，短天期債券易受政策利率
牽動，超長天期債券面臨財政赤字、國債供給及期限溢價等壓力，相較之下，中天期美債的風
險報酬較為均衡。信用債方面，投資等級企業基本面及現金流仍具韌性，後續仍可持續關注。

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 美國 9 月 ISM 非製造業 PMI 從 8 月的 55.4 降至 54.9，顯示服務業活動增速放
緩，然而國內需求強勁使供應鏈承壓，並推動投入價格指數由 72.6 升至 74.0 為
四年多來新高；另一方面，美國 10 月密西根大學消費者信心指數由 9 月的 48.1
降至 46.3，為連續第三個月下降且創 5 月以來最低，惟未來一年通膨預期由 4.6%
升至 4.7%，顯示通膨可能持續維持高位至 2027 年。
⚫ 聯準會 9 月 FOMC 會議紀要顯示與會官員均支持升息，並認為年底前尚需升息
一次，但對於後續升息時點及升息理由上存在分歧；其中部分官員認為升息是防
止能源及其他價格衝擊造成更廣泛影響的預防性措施，但更為鷹派官員則認為
升息在於抑制投資和支出，以防範正在顯現的需求驅動型通膨。
⚫ 歐洲央行 9 月貨幣政策會議紀要顯示全體決策委員均同意升息，並認為當前經
濟成長風險偏向下行，通膨風險則偏向上行，因此將保留政策彈性，根據經濟數

據做為決策，不提供未來利率路徑指引。
⚫ 台灣 9 月 CPI 年增率自 8 月 2.05%上升至 2.73%，已連續 5 個月超過 2%通膨警
戒線，且創兩年半來最大漲幅，除了豪雨導致蔬果價格上漲及中秋假期的短期因
素外，油價居高不下亦使通膨壓力增加。
⚫ 中國央行發布匯率政策立場聲明，強調中國貿易發展根源於產業國際競爭力的
提升，無必要通過匯率貶值獲取貿易競爭優勢，並指出中國長期實施以市場供需
為基礎、參考一籃子貨幣進行調節管理浮動匯率制度。

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Oct/12/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週台幣匯價高點在 31.699，低點在 31.940，以 31.875 作收，較前一週收盤價
貶值 4.5 分。 
上週台幣震盪走貶，週初，受到前週公布的美國非農就業數據低於預期，降低
市場對聯準會 10 月升息的預期，台股創高吸引外資匯入，推升台幣最高來到 31.699；
不過隨著美伊緊張情勢升溫且台灣連假將至，台股收低使外資轉為大幅賣超並匯出，
推低台幣回到 9 月中以來的震盪區間下緣，最低觸及 31.940，最終在央行調節之下
收在 31.875。 
＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣區間震盪。就國際市場而言，美國 10 月密西根大學消費者信心
指數持續下滑，由 9 月的 48.1 降至 46.3，創 5 月以來最低，惟未來一年通膨預期
升至 4.7%，加上中東局勢反覆，油價高位震盪，仍支撐市場對於聯準會年內升息
的預期，預計美元指數維持在 102 附近震盪，對台幣帶來壓力；就台灣市場來看，
台灣 9 月出口創歷史新高，年增 60.9%，反映 AI 及科技需求持續強勁，出口商後
續拋匯需求可望提供台幣支撐；然而，台股逼近五萬點關卡之際，外資未能延續買
超走勢，且連假前已大幅賣超台股，若後續外資持續匯出，將限制台幣升值空間。
綜合以上，預估本週台幣區間震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.700-32.000

美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Oct/12/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣區間震盪。中國十一長假期間 (10/1~10/7) 境內人民幣市場休市，而
境外人民幣維持偏強格局，主要在 6.7000 上方震盪，臨近周末，人行中間價為 6.7330，
創 3 年多來新高，加上美元指數漲勢放緩，推升境外人民幣突破 6.7000 整數關卡，
終場境外人民幣收在 6.6927，週線升值 134 點，境內人民幣收在 6.6929，較十一長
假前夕升值 121 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週人民幣區間偏升。就國際市場來看，美國密西根大學消費者信心指數
由 9 月的 48.1 降至 46.3，創 5 月以來新低，不過對於未來一年的通膨預期升至 4.7%，
加上油價維持高檔，市場對於聯準會年內升息的預期仍存，美元指數維持高檔震盪，
將限制人民幣升值速度；就中國國內市場來看，人行中間價維持偏強設定，不過人
行表示將防範短期大幅波動與急貶風險，並無意透過人民幣貶值取得貿易競爭優勢，
有助於穩定市場對人民幣的預期；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH 日線圖中，各天期
均線仍向下發散，空頭格局不變，下方支撐先看向 9 月底低點 6.6908。綜合以上，
預估本週人民幣區間偏升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6800-6.7100

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週 USD/JPY 震盪走升。週初，因美元指數在 102 附近盤整，帶領 USD/JPY 延
續前一週走勢震盪走升到 158 上方；週間，因缺乏重要經濟數據，市場偏向觀望，
USD/JPY 維持在 158 附近震盪；臨近週末，USD/JPY 曾一度回測 157.50 附近，不
過在美日利差的主導下，USD/JPY 依舊保持偏多的走勢，終場收在 158.31，週線上
漲 84 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、路透短觀調查顯示，受半導
體強勁訂單推動，日本 10 月大型製造業信心升至正 22，創近五年新高；但原料成
本高漲重擊非製造業，信心指數降至正 23。與此同時，此前投下升息反對票的日銀
鴿派委員佐藤綾野立場軟化，表態支持分階段升息，市場對後續正常化預期升溫，
預計將限縮 USD/JPY 的上行空間。二、日本首相高市早苗表示密切關注匯率與物價
走勢，強調日本已脫離通縮，將完全尊重央行政策獨立性，暗示不反對進一步升息。
面對通膨加劇與日圓走貶壓力，其過往堅持的貨幣寬鬆立場已出現轉向。然而目前
美日實質利差仍大，持續為 USD/JPY 提供支撐。三、就技術面而言，日線圖中，
USD/JPY 持續沿著上彎的月線上攻，然匯價持續承壓於季線下方，在有效站上季線
及突破前高壓力前，仍以區間震盪看待。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震
盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：157.10-159.50。

EUR/USD 下行最低來到 1.1160；週間，受中東地緣風險升溫與歐洲財政疑慮帶動避
險需求，加上美債殖利率維持高檔，美元利差優勢延續，歐元維持低檔；臨近週末，
逢低歐元有買盤支撐，帶動歐元收復部分跌幅，終場收在 1.1201，週線下跌 46 點。

  預估本週 EUR/USD 區間偏空震盪，主要原因如下：一、歐洲央行首席經濟學
家連恩表示，夏末能源成本飆升為經濟增長帶來下行風險，並可能限制央行收緊貨
幣政策的力道，與此同時，歐元區 10 月 Sentix 投資者信心指數由 5.1 降至 2.7，德
國預期指數同步滑落，反映市場對經濟復甦的信心明顯降溫，預計將令歐元持續承
壓。二、法國社會動盪與支出訴求阻礙財政減赤進程，加劇債務可持續性疑慮，並
推升法德公債利差與信用風險，令歐元承壓走弱。相較美國經濟展現韌性且聯準會
維持偏鷹立場，歐洲深陷景氣放緩與財政掣肘，歐洲央行緊縮空間受限。在歐美基
本面與政策前景持續分歧下，EUR/USD 缺乏實質支撐，後市將維持偏弱整理並下探
關鍵支撐。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 匯價雖多次向下測試 1.1165 關
鍵價位均獲得支撐，惟每次反彈均無法站上五日線，且各天期均線下彎呈現空頭排
列。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD 本週呈現區間偏空震盪的可能性較高。

### Page 1

Economic Indicators

2026 年 10 月30 日

1/3 
2026 年 7-9 月期 GDP 予測（1 次速報） 
～前期比年率＋0.4％と予想。成長率は鈍化も、景気回復の流れは崩れず～ 
経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト 新家義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

小幅ながら４四半期連続のプラス成長を予想、景気回復基調は維持 
11 月16 日に公表される2026 年７-９月期の実質ＧＤＰ成長率を前期比年率＋0.4％（前期比＋
0.1％）と予測する。４四半期連続のプラス成長になるだろう1。 
物価高による下押しや耐久財需要の反動に加え、天候不順の影響もあって個人消費が減少する
一方、設備投資が高い伸びになることが下支えになる見込みである。設備投資の伸びには特殊要
因も一部含まれているが、それを除いても堅調だったと考えられる。個人消費が弱含むなかでも
プラス成長を維持するとみられることは、企業部門の底堅さが景気を下支えしていることを示し
ている。過去３四半期に比較的高い成長が続いた後であることも踏まえれば、今回の減速をもっ
て景気の基調が弱まったと判断する必要はないだろう。家計部門の弱さは懸念材料だが、企業部
門の底堅さは維持されており、景気は引き続き緩やかな回復基調にあると評価できる。

輸入増による成長率押し下げと公的在庫のプラス寄与は表裏一体 
７-９月期のＧＤＰを評価する際には、原油輸入と公的在庫の動きをセットで捉える必要があ
る。４-６月期は、中東情勢の悪化によって原油輸入が激減したことが、ＧＤＰ統計上は輸入減を
通じて成長率を押し上げた。一方、輸入の減少による国内供給の不足を補うために国家備蓄原油
が放出され、公的在庫の取り崩しが進んだ。この結果、公的在庫変動は前期比寄与度▲0.5％Pt
（前期比年率寄与度▲2.1％Pt）と、成長率を大きく押し下げた。輸入減による成長率の押し上げ
は、公的在庫の減少と表裏一体の関係にあった。 
７-９月期は、これと逆の動きが生じるとみられる。政府・民間による代替調達の進展によって
原油輸入が回復するため、統計上は輸入増が成長率の押し下げ要因になる。一方、輸入の回復に
伴って国家備蓄原油の取り崩しは一服しており、公的在庫の減少幅は前期に比べて大きく縮小す
るとみられる。この結果、公的在庫変動は前期比寄与度＋0.4％Pt（前期比年率寄与度＋1.5％
Pt）と、成長率を大きく押し上げることが予想される。

1 10 月 30 日時点で入手可能な経済指標を元に作成している。国際収支や家計調査等、今後公表される経済指標の結果
を踏まえて予測値を修正する可能性がある。

### Page 2

2/3 
輸入増による成長率の押し下げと公的在庫変動による押し上げは、ともに原油の供給環境が改
善したことに伴う動きである。両者を個別に景気の強弱として評価することは適切ではなく、一
体として見るべきだろう。 
むしろ重要なのは、代替調達の進展によって国家備蓄への依存を減らしながら必要な原油を確
保できる状況になり、資源不足が生産活動を大きく制約するリスクが低下していることである。
ＧＤＰ統計上、輸入の増加は成長率の押し下げ要因となるものの、経済実態としては国内で利用
可能な資源の供給回復を意味している。この点は、７-９月期の日本経済を評価するうえで、むし
ろ前向きに捉えるべきだろう。

需要項目別の動向 
個人消費は前期比▲0.2％と小幅減少を予想する。８～９月に各地で相次いだ大雨や台風などの
天候不順が外出機会やサービス消費を抑制した可能性がある。また、４-６月期には自動車やエア
コンなどの耐久財販売が増加したが、７-９月期にはこうした一時的な押し上げ効果が剥落した。
加えて、物価高による節約志向が根強いなか、食料品や日用品などの非耐久財消費にも弱さがみ
られる。高い賃上げや底堅い雇用環境は下支え要因となっているものの、天候不順という一時的
な要因もあって個人消費は総じて力強さを欠く結果になったとみられる。 
設備投資は前期比＋2.0％と、高い伸びを予想する。４-６月期には特許権の海外譲渡に伴う研
究開発投資の減少という特殊要因があったが、７-９月期はその影響が剥落することが押し上げ要
因になった面はある。もっとも、こうした特殊要因を除いても設備投資は明確に増加したと考え
られ、底堅い動きとなっている。企業収益が高水準を維持するなか、人手不足に対応した省力化
投資やデジタル・ＡＩ関連投資への需要は根強く、企業の設備投資意欲は引き続き強い。設備投
資計画も高い伸びとなっており、設備投資は均してみれば緩やかな増加基調にあると判断してよ
いだろう。 
なお、特許権の海外への譲渡により、テクニカルに４-６月期はサービス輸出が上振れ、設備投
資が下振れたが、７-９月期はその剥落によりサービス輸出が下振れ、設備投資が上振れとなる見
込みである。両者は相殺し合うため、成長率全体でみれば概ねニュートラルとなる。 
輸出は前期比＋1.2％と４四半期連続の増加を予想する。４-６月期に大口要因によって研究開
発サービスの受取が急増した分が剥落することで、サービス輸出が減少する一方、財輸出が高い
伸びとなり、財貨・サービス輸出全体では増加を維持したとみられる。半導体製造装置などの資
本財輸出が好調だった。地域別では、ＥＵ向けが低調だった一方、ＡＳＥＡＮ、ＮＩＥｓ向けの
資本財輸出が伸びている。また、４-６月期に急減した中東向け輸出が持ち直したことも押し上げ
につながっている。 
輸入は前期比＋3.3％と大幅な増加が予想される。４-６月期には資源輸入の激減により輸入が
大幅に減少していたが、代替調達の進展により７-９月期は明確に持ち直した。この結果、外需寄
与度は前期比▲0.3％Pt（前期比年率▲1.3％Pt）になったと予想される。 
ただし、この外需寄与度のマイナスは、資源輸入の回復に伴う輸入の増加によって生じるもの

### Page 3

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。

である。その裏側では公的在庫変動が前期比＋0.4％ポイント、前期比年率では＋1.5％ポイント
の大幅プラス寄与になると見込まれる。両者は併せて評価する必要がある。 
民間在庫変動は前期比寄与度▲0.1％Ptと予想する。製品在庫はプラスが予想される一方、流通
在庫の下押しが大きくなるとみられる。 
住宅投資は前期比▲0.9％と、２四半期連続の減少を予想する。建設コストの上昇が下押し要因
となっており、住宅投資を取り巻く環境は引き続き厳しい。また、公共投資も同▲1.5％と減少を
予想するほか、政府消費についても、４-６月期の高い伸びの反動から前期比▲0.3％と減少を見
込む。

聯強(2347)：資金成本上揚壓抑短期營收動能，但長期營益率改善趨勢
不變 
建議：維持買進

一、聯強為亞太地區最大 3C 通
路商，營收成長以半導體業務最
佳  
 聯強為 3C 電子產品之銷售、配送、維修、量身訂做即時生產之專業通路商，為
亞太地區 3C 通路商龍頭，2026 年前三季產品比重分別為商用加值佔 31%、資
訊消費佔 17% 、 半 導 體 佔 48% 、 手 機 產 品 佔 4% ，成長主要來商用加值
(+112%YoY)和半導體業務(+162%YoY)。聯強海外市場包含中國、香港、紐澳、
泰國、印尼、印度及越南等地，其中以中國、台灣和印尼等區域較佳，營收分別
為 YoY+109%、YoY+78%和 YoY+18%。聯強競爭優勢為透過其四合一經營模
式，搭配其綿密的通路發揮規模經濟，並提供高附加價值的後勤運籌(庫存管理
與快速維為修服務等)，於中國主要競爭對手為神州數碼、天音通信控股。

二、半導體業務成長性佳，3Q26 
EPS 2.93 元創新高並優於預期 
 3Q26 營收 2366 億元(+18%QoQ；+136%YoY)優於預估值 14%，其中商用加
值和半導體營收分別為+116%YOY 和+273%YoY，因秉棄部分利潤不佳專案，
毛利率 3.49%優於前季之 3.4%，營業利益 61.41 億元(+28%%QoQ)優於預估
值 24%，因營運資金需求以及籌資成本上升致使利息費用提高至 9.34 億元
(+106%QoQ)，稅後純益 48.83 億元(+25%QoQ；+90%YoY)，EPS 2.93 元優
於預期之 2.5 元並創新高。

三、資金成本上揚導致業務拓展
較為謹慎，估計 4Q26 EPS 2.34
元 
 由於資金成本上揚致使公司放棄部分利潤不佳半導體業務，十月營收月減 34%，
估計 4Q26 營收 2046 億元(-14%QoQ；YoY+62%)，因產品組合變化轉佳，毛
利率提升至 3.58%，稅後純益 38.99 億元(-20%QoQ；+41%YoY)，EPS 2.34
元。

四、AI 長線需求有助商用加值和
半導體業務成長性 
 集邦 預估 2026 和 2027 年全 球九 大 CSP 資 本支 出分 別為 0.95 兆 美元
(+106%YoY)和 1.53 兆美元(+60%YoY)，且 AI 需求從雲端擴展到企業端，其中
以商用加值和半導體業務受惠幅度最大。 
在商用加值業務方面，在台灣和中國資料中心產品出貨挹注下，3Q26 營收
+116%YoY。展望 2027 年，中國十十五規劃和國產化政策推動政府和國企加速
採用本土品牌，推升聯強代理華為、浪潮等品牌業務成長性，台灣則是接獲數個
半導體大廠 AI 基礎建設專案，加計中小企業 AI 建置需求逐步顯現，商用業務將
為營運成長主要動能之一。 
在半導體業務方面，缺貨和漲價效致使記憶體、SSD 和硬碟業務為半導體業務
中 成 長 性 最 佳 者 ， 且 AI 推 論 亦 增 加 CPU 需求，3Q26 半 導 體 業 務 營 收

公司拜訪快報 
YoY+273%，半導體業務為營運成長另一重點業務，但因資金成本上揚，公司將
摒棄部分利潤未達目標專案，此將影響短期營收動能。 
受惠於出貨 ASP 增加，以及原廠擴大促銷力道挽救銷售量，3Q26 資訊家用和
手機業務分別+21%YoY 和+33%YoY。記憶體大漲致使原廠逐步反應成本於 PC
和手機新機售價，並將導致消費者延長換機週期，2027 年此兩塊業務成長性仍
相對保守看待。

五、資金成本上揚將致使接案更
為謹慎，但長期營益率好轉方向
不變 
 聯強今年接獲數個大型 AI 基礎建設專案，致使營收動能以及營運資金需求大增，
3Q26 遇到資金成本上揚，致使利息費用較前季倍增，公司將透過聯貸、可轉債
以及現增降低利息負擔，預期三項籌資工具皆完成後可望降低利息費用 20-
30%。展望 2027 年，儘管營收成長性不如 2026 年亮眼，但接單策略轉趨謹慎
將致使毛利率下滑壓力降低，且預期營業費用增幅有限下，營益率持續好轉方向
不變。

六、維持買進理由  1)預估 2026 和 2027 年 EPS 分別為 9.38 元和 11.07 元。 
2)儘管資金成本上揚壓抑短期營收動能，但長期營益率改善趨勢不變，維持買進
評等，TP 100 元 (2027 PE x 9)。

M
Update
Phison Electronics Corp  | Asia Pacific
3Q26 gross margin miss; our
conservative stance on Nand
modules playing out
Looking back to our February downgrade note: In AI boosts NAND demand, but
Asian module maker margins may soon compress, we noted that Phison's gross
margin could decline in 2Q26 or 3Q26, once it ran out of low-cost inventory.  While
Phison's low-cost inventory cushioned the consumer Nand module margin downside,
its overall NAND module margin started to miss in 3Q26, which means the AI

storage part of its business (eSSD, and cSSD for AI PC, together 40% of revenue)
may see margin decline.
Please see Exhibit 1  for our earnings review. The company's 3Q26 gross margin
declined to 59%, below our 63.5% and the street's 60.3%, also missing the
company's previous commentary about a sustainable gross margin in 3Q26 given its
low cost inventory. The company hopes its AiDaptive module and service business
can pick up and offset the margin pressure in the conventional module business in

the coming quarters.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Document 117: 261109_daiwa_ Giant Manufacturing

Source PDF: all report pdf/261109_daiwa_ Giant Manufacturing.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-09
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Consumer Discretionary

What’s new: Giant announced its 3Q26 results after market hours on 9 
November 2026. We reaffirm our Buy (1) rating on its improving 
fundamentals and the recovery of the global bike market. We believe 
inventory destocking is coming to an end.

What’s the impact: 3Q26 results: missed on operating margin. Giant’s 
3Q26 revenue of TWD16.311bn (-2.1% QoQ, +6.2% YoY) was roughly in 
line with our estimate and the Bloomberg consensus. Its gross margin of 
23.9% beat our estimate and the Bloomberg consensus by 2.9pp and 
1.1pp, respectively, driven by product mix optimisation and new product 
launches in its own brand. However, its operating margin of 3.1% missed 
our estimate and the consensus by 1.4pp and 2.1pp, respectively, due to 
marketing activities for MY27 launches and legal fees related to the WRO. 
Thus, its 3Q26 operating profit of TWD510m (-49.0% QoQ, +28.9% YoY) 
missed our estimate and the consensus by 31.3% and 42.2%, respectively. 
Its 3Q26 net profit of TWD330m (-48.0% QoQ, -5.7% YoY) also missed our 
estimate and the consensus by 39.6% and 47.8%, respectively. Its 3Q26 
EPS finished at TWD0.84.

Demand recovery in Europe and China; a bright 2027. Giant sees 
gradual market recovery in Europe on new model launches and demand 
recovery in China. In the US, Giant continues to work towards having the 
WRO lifted, but will adjust production allocation to maximise the supply to 
meet demand in the US market. Its E-bike revenue accounted for 21% of 
9M26 revenue, which we believe reflects the declining OEM segment. 
Giant aims for its e-bike shipments to decline by 1% YoY, from 516,000 
units in 2025 to 510,000 in 2026, on weaker OE customer demand. For 
2026, it guides for sales to decline by low-single-digit % YoY, with the gross 
margin improving to 21-23% (from 20-21%) and an operating margin of 1-
3%. We lower our 2026-28E EPS by 5-30% after factoring in the 3Q26 
results with more conservative operating-margin estimates on marketing 
expenses.

What we recommend: We reaffirm our Buy (1) call with an unchanged 
12M TP of TWD121, still based on our 1-year forward EPS forecast with an 
unchanged target PER of 22x to reflect its improved earnings trajectory, 
and we look for a better year in 2027. Key downside risks: slower-than-
expected e-bike penetration in the EU and macro/tariff uncertainties.

How we differ: Our 2026-28E EPS are 8-26% below the consensus, likely 
as our revenue and operating margin forecasts are more conservative.

9 November 2026

3Q26 results review: missed on operating margin

➢ 3Q26 net profit missed our estimate and the consensus by 39-48% 
➢ Better-than-expected recovery in Europe and China; a brighter 2027 
➢ Reaffirming our Buy (1) rating with an unchanged 12M TP of TWD121

Company profile 
Founded in 1972 and listed in 1994 on Taiwan’s main board, Giant is the largest bicycle maker in 
Taiwan, with an annual capacity of c.5m bicycles. In 2025, Asia accounted for 57% of its revenue, 
followed by Europe (33%) and America (7%). Around 74% of its 2025 revenue came from OBM (Giant, 
Liv, momentum) brands and 26% from OEM/ODM (Trek, Scott and Colnago) brands.

### Page 5

5

Giant Manufacturing (9921 TT): 9 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 7 Aug 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
The CEO and Chairperson are not the same individual. Led by the Liu family, there are 11
members on the board. The founder and former Chairman is now a director, with his niece, Mrs.
Tu, leading Giant. We view Mrs. Tu's leadership to be strong, as she is well-recognised by the
former Chairman and the board for her capabilities, and she is one of the few female
chairpersons to lead a Taiwanese company.
Capital management 2 Giant has maintained its dividend payout ratio between 50% and 100% over the past decade , which we believe to be reasonable considering its global leadership status and expansion needs.
Related party &
transaction 2
Revenue from related-party transactions accounted for 58-62% of total revenue over 2019-25,
with sales mostly from its OBM (ASP varies with products/markets). We observed no suspicious
activity.
S Product quality & safety 1
According to Giant's 2023 ESG report, Giant as the global OEM/OBM leader in the bicycle
industry, product quality and safety are the top priority. We see ongoing R&D expenses and
relevant investment in new models and note that Giant has introduced its "Right Ride" system to
assist customers seeking suitable bike options.
S Supply chain management 1
Giant has developed its Giant production management system that diversifies its capacity
globally and utilises production resources fully to lower political or regional risk. As Giant has
control over its material sourcing, design process and manufacturing/OEM, we believe it has
solid supply chain management capabilities that allow for better cost control than peers.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

### Page 1

All Ring

Accelerated advanced packaging and CPO 
ramp optionality at attractive valuation

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 1,750 TWD | Price: 1,025 TWD

2H26 strength sustains on 2.5D packaging expansion 
3Q26 sales were +41% QoQ above Street estimates on solid CoWoS and FOCoS capacity 
expansion across its foundry and OSAT customers, backed by the accelerated capex from 
back-end industry to accommodate stronger cloud AI chipset demand outlook into ‘27. 
With mix shifted toward margin-diluted automation tools, 3Q26 GMs were -630 bps QoQ 
to 48.5% (vs. BofAe 54.1%) though OpMs held resilient QoQ at 32.9% on operating 
leverage, taking 3Q26 EPS to NT$8.50 (vs. BofAe/Street NT$10.74/NT$9.34.). For 4Q26, 
we estimate sales -9% QoQ due to CoWoS revenue recognition timing partially offset by 
sustained strength in FOCoS. We expect GMs in 4Q26 to recover to its long-term guidance 
of 50-55% range (BofAe 53.7%) on better product mix.   
Diverse 2.5D/3D packaging and CPO ramp lifting ‘27-28 
All Ring’s sales are on track to +88% YoY in ‘26 and could see continued strength into ‘27 
(BofAe +55% YoY), mainly led by 80-90k WPM CoWoS addition from TSMC to 200-210k 
WPM by 4Q27 along with ASE’s accelerated FOCoS capacity expansion and CoWoS full 
process ramp for outsourced business. With superior throughput and yield stability vs. its 
peers, we expect its CPO optical coupling equipment shipment to reach 150 units in ‘27, 
backed by a solid commitment from Nvidia/SPIL to increase CPO assembly capacity by 15x 
(see our report: Implication from Nvidia GTC: back-end capacity constrained; CPO 
accelerates). Broader equipment share and content gains across diversifying advanced 
packaging technologies, including FOPLP, SoIC, CoPoS and US back-end solutions, 
alongside the ramp-up of CPO equipment should drive All Ring’s advanced packaging 
revenue +60% YoY in 2027, lifting GM/OpM to 54.2%/33.3% from 52%/32.2% in 2026.  
Reiterate Buy on decent EPS upcycle with +34% CAGR 
We maintain our 2027/28 EPS unchanged at NT$49.34/54.67, our Buy rating on All Ring 
and PO of NT$1,750 still based on 36x 2027E P/E (vs. its 8-41x range). We believe 
decent sales/earnings CAGR of +30%/+34% over '26-'28E supported by solid advanced 
packaging capacity build and smooth CPO ramp should warrant high valuation.

Company Sector 
Semiconductor Capital Equipment

Company Description 
Founded in 1996, All Ring is a Taiwan-based semiconductor 
equipment supplier with core technologies spanning 
dispensing, attachment, AOI, optical coupling, and precision 
automation. Building on its initial expertise in passive 
component manufacturing equipment, the company has 
gradually expanded into the back-end semiconductor 
packaging market and established long-term partnerships 
with leading foundry and OSATs customers.

Investment Rationale 
We have a Buy rating on All Ring, in view of its solid market 
position in the semiconductor advanced packaging 
equipment. We believe its long-standing relationships with 
leading foundry and OSATs, coupled with strong technology 
development capabilities, position the company well to 
capture the secular uptrend in AI-driven advanced 
packaging capacity expansion and rising equipment 
content.

### Page 4

Price objective basis & risk 
All Ring (XNQNF) 
Our PO of NT$1,750 is based on 36x 2027E P/E, positioned at the upper half of its 
historical trading range of 8-41x. We believe P/E based valuation is appropriate given All 
Ring's robust top-line visibility, stable profitability profile, and structural earnings 
improvement on decent exposure to AI-driven advanced packaging upcycle. The 
valuation multiple is underpinned by 
1) 34%/37% CAGR in sales and earnings over 2026-28E and 2) sustained ROE in the 30-
40% range

Downside risks are: 
1) slowdown in advanced packaging capacity expansion and 2) mis-execution on 
equipment development

2026/11/6 收盤價： 2,830.00 ➢ 26Q3 營收 260.5 億元，QoQ+241.0%，YoY+296.5%，
創單季歷史新高，主因 N3 專案量產出貨。毛利率
18.0%(QoQ-16.8pt，YoY-10.0pt)，因量產營收比重大
增、部分高毛利 NRE 認列遞延，26Q3 毛利率已接近產
品組合的最差情況。EPS 37.71元。 
➢ 26Q4預期營收再創歷史新高，另有重大專案 tape-out認
列；26Q4毛利率預期與 26Q3 相近，2026全年毛利率目
標維持 20%以上。

預期本世代量產約 2 年，為世芯 2026~2027 年最大營收
來源。下一代 N2專案已進入最後階段，26Q4 tape out，
2028年量產。 
➢ 另外取得第 2大客戶為美國知名大廠(非中國客戶)，為世
芯首次合作的新客戶，直接與客戶合作、無其他第三方。
預計 27Q2起量產營收貢獻，專案營收規模約 10億美元。 
➢ 調整預估 2026/2027 營收 676 億元/1,373 億元，

營 運 分 析

世芯為具先進製程能力的 Design Service 公司 
世芯電子(Alchip)成立於 2003 年，於 2014 年掛牌上市，為全球少數能提供
7nm先進製程以下 ASIC back end 設計服務的公司，成立至今已協助全球客
戶完成超過 470 款的晶片設計，並且為全球晶圓代工龍頭台積電 VCA 聯盟
的合作夥伴，以及台積電 3DFabric聯盟的成員。以應用別區分 26Q3 營收， 
Consumer 佔比 8%、Networking佔比 5%、Niche佔比 0%、HPC佔比 87%，
以製程別來看，26Q3營收比重：3nm以下先進製程佔 80%、7/5nm先進製
程佔比 15%、12/16nm 佔比 3%、28nm佔比 1%。

圖二、26H2毛利率回落 
世芯各季營收及獲利走勢預估(百萬元)

資料來源：世芯；兆豐投顧預估

N3放量出貨，26Q4 N2 tape out，2027新增第 2大客戶 
N3專案 26Q3 進入量產，晶片測試結果與良率符合預期，預期本世代量產約
2 年，為世芯 2026~2027 年最大營收來源。公司預期 2027 年 N3 專案營收
呈三位數(>100%)成長。下一代 N2專案已進入最後階段，26Q4 tape out，
預計 27Q4 進入試產，2028年量產。世芯是唯一實體設計供應商，且客戶承
諾 N2僅客戶本身和世芯向台積電下單，2028年營收將進入新成長階段。 
另外取得第 2大客戶：合約已簽署、專案已啟動，客戶為美國知名大廠(非中
國客戶)，為世芯首次合作的新客戶，直接與客戶合作、無其他第三方。預計
27Q2起量產營收貢獻，專案營收規模約 10億美元，2027年起成為世芯第 2
大營收來源。公司表示這是新商業模式的開端，預期後續有更多專案。 
ASIC產業方面，COT(Customer Owned Tooling)為長期趨勢，由客戶負責
前端設計和架構，後段委託其他廠商。公司認為此趨勢有助於發揮世芯的優
勢，預估近期將取得更多重要專案。潛在客戶方面，公司表示 Google 是爭
取目標，對贏得此客戶非常樂觀且有高度信心，但未透露合作型態。 
車用電子方面，中國自駕、ADAS 項目需求強勁，已成為世芯 2026 年第 2
大營收來源，客戶下一代設計預計 26Q4 tape out，符合進度。 
增員方面，26Q3營業費用 10.7億元，QoQ+5.4%，營業費用率降至 4.1%，
營運槓桿顯現。公司導入 AI設計流程，前端驗證與報告分析可節省專案中間
階段人力，但最終階段仍需大量資源。此外，公司聘請前 Nvidia高層 Freddy
擔任 CBO，Freddy過去在 Nvidia、Xilinx有資料中心和 Hyperscaler 相關經
驗，聘請 Freddy加強和北美客戶的長期夥伴關係。

2027年營收可望翻倍 
公司表示 2027 年營收翻倍到 45~50 億美元是可達成的目標。我們調整預估

기아 (000270)

Company Brief 
자동차/모빌리티 
Analyst 김준성 
joonsung.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 배현서 
hyeonseo.bae@meritz.co.kr 배당은 지켜질 것  
3Q26 지배주주 순이익, 컨센서스 -38% 하회 
매출 31.6조원 (+10% YoY)으로 컨센서스에 부합했다. 출고 판매 (+10% YoY), 소
매 판매 (+8% YoY) 모두 높은 성장을 기록하며, 외형 확대에 기여했다. 
영업이익 1.82조원 (+24% YoY)으로 컨센서스를 -25% 하회했다. 전년 동기 대미 
관세 25% 부과에 따른 기저효과 +5,330억원 (2025년 11월부터 15%로 하향)이 
존재했고 기말 환율 (원/달러 -12% QoQ 강세) 변동에 따른 판매보증 충당부채 환
입 +6,700억원을 인식했음에도 불구하고, 신규 출시 저가 BEV 모델들의 판매 확대
에 따른 전사 평균 판매가격 하락  할인 경쟁 심화  원재료 및 운송비 상승으로 부
진한 실적을 기록했다. 
지배주주 순이익 1.29조원 (-10% YoY)으로 컨센서스를 -38% 하회했다. 영업 실적 
부진과 함께 원화 강세에 의한 외화자산 평가손실 및 관계사 실적 악화에 따른 지분
법 이익 감소 (-36% YoY)가 더해진 결과다.

6,000원 이상 배당 유지 전망 
4Q26 이후 실적 방향도 먹구름이 가득하다. 원/달러 환율의 YoY 강세 반영이 시작
된다. 미국 시장 내 경쟁력 확보를 위해 가장 중요한 원/엔 환율의 방향성 또한 비우
호적 흐름이 지속 중이다. 내수 시장을 포함해 전세계 주요 시장 내 중국산 차량과의 
경쟁 강도도 높아지고 있다. 원유 가격 뿐만 아니라 알루미늄, 구리 등 핵심 원재료 
가격의 상승 반영도 이어지고 있다. 
2026년 연간 지배주주 순이익 6.9조원 (-9% YoY)을 전망한다. 주주환원 총액은 축
소될 것이다. 다만 자사주 매입을 줄이더라도 배당은 전년 수준을 지킬 것으로 기대
한다. 참고로 2025년 실적에 기반한 주주환원은 자사주 매입 0.9조원, 배당 2.6조원 
(주당 6,800원)이었으며, 주주환원율은 48%였다. 현대차그룹은 Boston Dynamics
에 대한 증자를 이어가고 있다. 정의선 회장도 마찬가지다. 그리고 기아의 배당은 현
대차  현대글로비스의 배당과 함께 증자 재원으로 활용되고 있다. 6,000원 이상의 
배당 유지를 기대하며, 6% 이상 시가 배당 수익률 제시를 통해 주가 하방 경직성 확
보가 가능하다고 판단한다. 기업가치 상승 재개를 위해서는 기존 차량 제조  판매 사
업을 넘어 피지컬 데이터 디바이스 제공 사업 진출 가시성 확보가 필요하다. 
 Meritz Research  2026. 11. 2

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준) 
적정주가 (12개월) 150,000원 
현재주가 (10.30) 95,600원 
상승여력 56.9%

3Q26 OP 2.7조, 당사 추정치 35% 상회 
서프라이즈의 근거가 판매보증비 감소에서 발생. 3Q26 기말환율의 낙폭 (-12% qoq)으로 
인한 역설적 비용절감의 규모가 전년동기 대비 1.2조 원을 넘어서며 환율의 조사모삼 효과
로 만들어진 컨센서스 상회 실적. 
긍정적인 부분: 컨틴전시, 신차 효과, 인센티브, 생산 정상화 
지속적인 컨틴전시 체제를 유지하며 비용절감 실천 (=지속된 요소). 4분기 추가적으로 나아
질 요인은 1) 내수 신차효과로 판가 상승 및 유기적 수익성 개선, 2) 공장 가동률 정상화를 
통한 고정비 부담 절감, 3) 해외시장 신차효과를 통한 딜러 인센티브 절감. 
경계할 부분: 재료비, 환율, BEV 판매비중 확대 
재료비 측면에서 1) 2Q26 시작된 미국-이란 전쟁 관련 원재료비 상승은 4Q26F에도 하락
세를 보이지 못할 것이며, 2) 구리, 철강 등의 금속 원자재의 가격 상승 또한 4Q26F 지속될 
것. 또한 3Q26과 반대로 qoq 기말환율 대비 낙폭 확대할 평균환율 (mtd 1,338원) 효과로 
환 관련 감익 본격화, IONIQ 3의 출시는 인센티브 절감인 동시에 믹스 관점에서 부정적. 
투자의견 BUY 유지, 목표주가 440,000원으로 소폭 상향 
목표주가 상향 근거는 컨틴전시 체제 유지를 통한 FY27F 자동차사업 부문 이익 예상치 상
향. 부정적 외생변수 (환율, 재료비)가 지속된다는 전제 하에 FY27F OPM 하락을 예상하나, 
(FY26F 5.6%, FY27F 5.2%) 향후 신차효과와 생산 정상화에 따른 자동차 사업부문 수익성 
반등의 여력 존재. 주가 상승 트리거는 1) CES 전후 BD 관련 기대감, 2) 신차 출시 이후 견
조한 판매량, 3) 환율, 원재료비 등 외생변수의 개선.

### Page 1

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Oil Price Dynamics Remain Key to the Outlook
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
Inflation, interest rates and equity market dynamics have changed materially since mid-
August, as widening attacks on Middle East and Russian crude oil and refining capacity
worsened supply and price conditions. Indeed, with the oil market already strained, an
early-September attack on a major Saudi Arabian export pipeline sharply raised concerns

about a protracted and deepening oil crisis. While Middle East crude exports eventually
managed to significantly rebound, increased security risks and freight costs raised
physical crude prices.
Against this backdrop, the spot Brent price surged from $92/barrel in mid-August
back into a $115 to $135/barrel price range until recently. Reflecting unusual market
tightness, other North Sea oil grades were recently trading even higher, up to $160/
barrel, according to the International Energy Agency (IEA). Even as it cooled from $135

to $115/barrel between October 2 and October 20, spot Brent is still almost double
year-ago levels, and so are many diesel prices. The AAA average U.S. retail diesel price
recently hit a new record and was 74% higher year-over-year (YoY) as of October 20.
With insufficient regional supply, the New York Harbor wholesale diesel price is 130%
higher from a year ago, according to the Energy Information Administration (EIA).
Indeed, sharply higher oil prices and a growing diesel shortage have put the global diesel

market under extreme strain. The U.S., for example, has little distillate cushion and
limited spare refining capacity. The distillate inventory (diesel and fuel oil) has decreased
from 37 days of demand coverage in January to just 26 days by early October, according
to the EIA. This 30% drop brought the supply buffer to the bottom of its historical
range. With limited refining capacity and reliance on imports for about 10% of regional
consumption, East Coast distillate inventories are the lowest since 1990, for example,

and are expected to remain below their 5-year average into late 2027, according to EIA
data. More broadly, according to the IEA’s October Oil Market Report (OMR), “oil market
balances are forecast to remain in deficit through the end of the year, with constrained
middle distillate supplies likely to persist well into 2027.”
Thus, although wage growth/labor demand do not appear inflationary, the oil crisis
risks continuing to feed “core” inflation. According to the Federal Reserve’s Beige Book1

covering precisely the mid-August to early-October period of heightened oil-market
turmoil, “higher oil prices permeated all District reports,” with firms noting that fuel
surcharges were passing on increased costs throughout the supply chain. The 22% rise
in airfares over the past year alone contributed around 0.2 percentage point (pp) to the
2.6% 12-month increase in the “core” consumer price index (CPI) for September. Aside
from transportation costs effects, the spike in diesel prices also portends high food

prices with a few-month lag given planting/harvesting cost considerations.
By solidifying expectations for higher Fed interest rates to fight inflation, the surge in oil
prices has contributed to the spike in long-term Treasury yields this year. According to an
October 13, 2026, Empirical Research Partners report, the Iran-war oil crisis is estimated
to have contributed about 0.50 pp to the roughly 2.40 pp increase in the real U.S. long-
term Treasury rate from 2020 to early October (Exhibit 1A). According to the report, this

oil-price effect accounts for “much of the rise in real rates since February 27, 2026...”
On the positive side, while most other underpinnings for higher rates compared to the
pre-pandemic decade are structural (Exhibit 1A), the oil-price-related effect on interest
rates is reversible, implying that a resolution to the current oil crisis has the potential to
help lower both inflation and interest rates, relieving some of the current headwinds to
growth and asset prices.

Indeed, while much of the increase in interest rates since the pandemic reflects stronger,
more normal economic growth, and has thus been accompanied by surging corporate
1 Summary of Commentary on Current Economic Conditions by Federal Reserve District, Federal Reserve, October 2026.
Investment Implications
Oil market conditions remain a swing
factor for the outlook. A shift lower in
oil risk premia and improvement on
the refining front would go a long way

toward easing inflation and interest
rates while bolstering risk-asset
appetite. A gradual increase in Fixed
Income portfolio duration continues to
make sense, in our view.

3 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
earnings and risk-asset prices, worsening oil-market conditions since mid-summer and
associated spike in yields have been accompanied by a loss of momentum in U.S. equity
prices (Exhibit 1B). Given their higher sensitivity to energy price and interest rates shocks,
Small-caps lost relatively more momentum than Large-caps during this period.
The Large-cap rally also faded, though, as oil and interest rate headwinds widened stock-

level return dispersion, with roughly 40% of constituent stocks in bear market territory
(down 20% or more). Still, buoyed by the Technology sector as well as by expectations
of conflict resolution, the market-capitalization weighted S&P 500 Index has mainly
consolidated just below its peak. While the equal-weighted S&P 500 Index stumbled more,
it was just about 3% off its peak as of October 20.
While the economy remains resilient and profit expectations remain strong, persistent

oil market turmoil makes the macro backdrop more fragile. For example, the Beige Book
not only highlights concern about spreading fuel-related inflation, as noted above, but its
language also suggests that economic activity lost some momentum since its mid-July
to late-August report. Overall, it describes growth as slight to moderate, with generally
moderate manufacturing growth except for robust activity in the energy-heavy Dallas
District. Little growth is reported in other sectors overall, with most Districts seeing little

to no change in consumer spending, for example.
Employment was described as stable to little changed, with generally adequate labor supply
except for certain specialized trade workers. Five of twelve Districts note some reports of
businesses cutting staff through attrition, delaying hiring or anticipating less job creation
as a result of AI-driven productivity gains. Most Districts reported that business maintained
a positive outlook for coming months, though “expectations tend to be for slight growth,

weighted toward manufacturing and burdened by persistent uncertainty.”
The September employment and retail sales reports seem to validate the anecdotal Beige
Book evidence. Also, the 0.4% annualized manufacturing production growth over the July to
September period (and 1.1% YoY, according to the Federal Reserve Broad), while welcome,
is hardly an indication of booming conditions. Against a backdrop of rising electricity prices,
wage growth lagging inflation, and higher gasoline prices broadly offsetting this year’s tax

cut benefits, real consumer spending is unlikely to accelerate much absent stronger labor
demand and real wage growth. A resolution to the oil crisis that brings economic relief and
tempers interest rates is thus critical for continued expansion in 2027, in our view.

Investment Implications
Portfolio maintenance is a key
component of successful investing.
It’s time for investors to assess their
portfolios—or look under the hood.
MARKET VIEW
Six Questions Investors Should Be Asking Themselves Right Now
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
After four years of rising equity prices, investors have plenty to celebrate. Despite

inflation scares, geopolitical tensions, trade wars, rising interest rates and breathless
predictions around AI, the S&P 500 has returned some 131% (total returns) since the
bull market in equities commenced in October 2022. Corporate earnings have generally
exceeded expectations; AI has ignited a historic investment boom, and America’s
technology giants have powered the market to new heights.
We believe the bull will rumble on into 2027. We also believe that now is the time for

investors to take stock of their portfolios, since bull markets have a way of changing the
composition of portfolios. In addition, investors should not assume that the investment
strategies that worked well in the past four years will necessarily deliver comparable
results during the next four. Below are six questions investors should be asking
themselves right now:
Number One: How concentrated is my portfolio? Many investors believe they are
invested in the stock market when, in fact, they are invested in a handful of companies—

specifically AI-related names that have soared and dominated the index over the past
few years. Information technology now accounts for roughly 40% of the market cap of
the S&P 500, while the top 10 companies represent a share of 38.4%.
As the Financial Times recently noted, U.S. equity and credit markets have become so
increasingly concentrated in AI-related groups that “more than two-thirds of companies
in the Russell 1000 index of large U.S. groups are linked to AI, either directly or

indirectly or because the technology is becoming embedded in their business models.”2
Meanwhile, Big Technology hyperscalers and companies that are beneficiaries of the AI
spending boom make up about 16% of the U.S. high-grade bond market after a huge
borrowing binge, making the grouping the largest in the investment-grade bond market.2
The upshot: Investors that have been rewarded by concentration need to ask themselves
what percentage of their wealth is tied to the all-encompassing AI trade and consider

what would happen if mega-cap tech leadership falters.
Number Two: How diversified is my portfolio? While concentration asks whether
investors own too much of a few assets, diversification asks whether investors own enough
of different assets that behave differently when economic cycles or financial conditions
change. A portfolio containing hundreds of U.S. Equities via exchange-traded funds, mutual
funds and other assets may appear diversified. But if those companies are overwhelmingly

exposed to the same economic cycle, interest rate environment, or technology theme,
the diversification benefits are diluted. Case in point: While we remain constructive on
Emerging Market (EM) Equities, investors need to understand that a significant driver of
EM returns has come from AI-driven markets like Taiwan and South Korea.
Today, true diversification means considering exposure to hard assets (commodities, real
estate), EM mining and agricultural leaders, EM bonds and currencies, China bio-technology,

Small-cap international, and Private Equity and Hedge Funds for qualified investors.
Number Three: How tax-efficient is my portfolio? Bull markets create wealth—in
addition to tax liabilities. The good news is that after four years of rising equity returns,
investors are sitting on sizable, unrealized capital gains. The bad news: Market gains
can feel like a financial straitjacket, making it difficult to rebalance portfolios without
triggering significant tax liabilities.

Against this backdrop, now is the time for investors to review where the assets are
held—distinguishing between taxable and tax-advantaged accounts, and the attendant tax
ramifications. The appropriate mix depends on individual circumstances, including future
2 “Investors seek shelter from heightened exposure to AI”, The Financial Times, October 6, 2026.

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
withdrawal taxes. It is also time to consider tax-loss strategies, charitable donations of
securities and other tactics and strategies since in the end, the measure of investment
success isn’t simply how much money investors make. It’s how much they keep.
Number Four: Is it time to take profits?* There is a peculiarity to bull markets: The
more money investors make, the more the attendant tax liabilities. Tax liabilities, in turn,

can discourage selling, and the reluctance to sell can leave investors dangerously exposed
to yesterday’s leaders and compound concentration risks. That brings up an emotionally
difficult decision: When should investors sell their leaders or take some chips off the table?
The question is notably relevant today, given four years of substantial Equity gains.
Beyond tax considerations, investors need to understand that profit-taking can create
financial flexibility. Realized after-tax gains can replenish cash reserves, fund near-

term spending needs, reduce leverage or most importantly, provide capital to rebalance
portfolios in other areas of the market. In general, the goal of taking profits isn’t to get
out of the market—it is to ensure that after years of accumulated gains, these proceeds
are redeployed and redistributed across various asset classes that continue to support
and underpin long-term wealth accumulation.
Number Five: How well is my portfolio positioned for a new interest rate

regime? For much of the past quarter century, investors operated in a world of copious
liquidity, declining interest rates and relatively cheap capital. The era of capital abundance
favored stocks over bonds, but that era is over—we are shifting to a world of capital
scarcity, or intense competition for capital and the attendant hike in the cost of capital.
In this world, higher rates weigh the most on long-duration assets. Positioning for this
regime favors companies with strong balance sheets and cash flow over firms with heavy

debt or refinancing needs. High-quality bonds are more attractive in this environment;
sovereign debt risks are concentrated more overseas (i.e., France) than the U.S.
Number Six: How well is my portfolio positioned for the next shock? Shocks
happen. If the past four years have taught investors anything, it is that the unexpected
should be the expected. This decade alone has produced gut-wrenching market moves
related to a global pandemic, a ground war in Europe, the reconfiguration of global

supply chains, epic technological change and a war in the Middle East. Through it all, U.S.
Equities have trended higher but hardly in a linear fashion.
The next shocks could emerge in the global bond market, where mounting government
debt and higher borrowing costs have triggered mounting volatility. It could emerge from
disappointing returns on massive AI investments. Or it could come from a geopolitical
event, another energy shock or any other unforeseen event. Investors can’t predict the

next shock, but they can prepare for it. So now is the time for investors to stress test
their portfolios against a potential shock, examining how a steep downdraft in Equities
or spike in bond yields would affect their retirement plans, liquidity needs and other
financial goals and objectives.
Exhibit 2: Six Questions to Stress Test Your Portfolio.
The six questions What investors should examine Portfolio diagnostic
1. How concentrated am I? Mega-cap technology exposure and overlapping holdings % of portfolio in top 10 stocks

2. Am I truly diversified? Asset allocation, geographic mix and correlated exposures Current equity/bond mix versus target
3. How tax-efficient am I? Unrealized gains, asset location and tax-loss harvesting Embedded capital gains and estimated tax cost of rebalancing
4. Is it time to take profits? Portfolio drift, oversized winners and valuations Positions exceeding target allocation
5. Am I ready for higher interest rates? Duration, leverage, refinancing risks and cash flows Bond duration and exposure to highly leveraged companies

6. Am I prepared for the next shock? Liquidity, downside exposure and portfolio resilience Estimated loss in a 20% equity selloff

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What are rising credit spreads signaling about the broader market environment?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Officer
Rising French bond yields with a sharp rise in spreads above German bunds, mixed
with AI buildout concerns, are creating nervousness across the capital markets. This
nervousness centers around the ability to fund deficits, the potential for slower growth

and the future return on the announced capital investment. We expect these concerns
to continue to linger well into next year as growth worries remain in France, bond
yields stay sticky around current levels, and, in the case of AI, investors demand more
compensation for financing.
At this stage, we do not expect the rising spreads in targeted areas to be a precursor
for a material slowdown in economic growth overall. Rather, we believe this is a natural

signal that investors are moving up the quality stack as we move into the middle of
the AI buildout. The bottom line is that rising credit spreads in lower quality corners of
the market are natural along the lifecycle of financing. When free cash flow is called
into question, or when debt financing costs begin to approach the expected return on
investment, investors begin to reprice risk.
We continue to emphasize higher quality balance sheets in corporate and sovereign

credit as well as higher quality leaders in the AI buildout overall. The closer we get to
2027, investors will likely begin to transition from too much exposure to the “buildout”
and not enough exposure to “AI adopters.”

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
SS&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

Consumer Price Index is a statistical measure that tracks the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a representative "market basket" of everyday goods and
services.
S&P 500 Market-cap Weighted Index is a float-adjusted market-capitalization-weighted index, meaning a company's influence and percentage weight in the index are determined by its
total market value (share price multiplied by the number of outstanding shares).

S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index is a variation of the traditional S&P 500 where every single company in the index is given the exact same weight—0.2%—regardless of its size or market
capitalization.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Industrials Index imposes equal weights on the index constituents included in the S&P 500 that are classified in the GICS® industrials sector.
Russell 1000 Index is a stock market index that tracks the performance of the 1,000 largest publicly traded companies in the United States.

or sale of any security or investment strategy. Merrill offers a broad range of brokerage, investment advisory and other services. There are important differences between brokerage and

of all investors.
Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

FOLLOWING THE OIL SHOCK, EL NIÑO THREATENS TO HURT MACROECONOMIC RESILIENCE IN SUB-SAHARAN
AFRICA FURTHER
Lucas Plé
Seven months after the start of the war in the Middle East, which has led to a sharp rise in oil prices, sub-Saharan Africa is holding up well
overall. The deterioration in external accounts and the rise in inflation are more contained than in 2022, during the last oil shock. However,
this resilience is fragile and is eroding as the conflict drags on. Furthermore, an exceptional El Niño event could significantly worsen the

macroeconomic environment in 2027 1.
Economic growth remains robust
For countries with available data, GDP growth held up well in the first half of 2026 2. In mid-October, the IMF revised its regional forecast for
the full year upwards to 4.5% (+0.2 percentage points compared with April), in line with the performance for 2025.
1 El Niño 2026–2027: multiple pressures on prices and supplies
2 1.4% year-on-year (y/y) in South Africa, 4.2% in Nigeria, 5.1% in Kenya, 7% in Angola, 7.3% in Ghana.

Sub-Saharan Africa is highly vulnerable to climate shocks via the agricultural commodities channel

### Page 2

2
The bank for
a changing
world
Chart of the Week / 21 October 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
A mitigated shock on terms of trade
High prices for export commodities (gold, copper, platinum) have helped to cushion the impact on terms of trade and the external
accounts of hydrocarbon-importing countries. For the 10 countries in our sample (see heatmap), the depreciation of their currencies
against the US dollar since last February has been moderate overall (2.3% on average).

Inflation has started to rise again, but it is still being contained so far
In the ten countries in our sample, although inflation is rising again, it is lower year-on-year for the first eight months of 2026 than it was
for the same period in 2022. This is due to:
1. Monetary policies that were still restrictive before the conflict started;
2. Fuel subsidies;
3.   Macroeconomic reforms implemented in recent years (a return to orthodox monetary policy in Nigeria, and efforts towards fiscal

consolidation in many countries);
4. Moderate currency depreciation since February.
As a result, few central banks have tightened their monetary policy since February (South Africa, Uganda and Tanzania).
However, this resilience is still fragile…
On the one hand, governments that have chosen to cap fuel price rises are seeing their public finances deteriorate. In Senegal, energy
subsidies will go above 3% of GDP in 2026, which is unsustainable. On the other hand, the region’s external accounts are exposed to

volatile global commodity prices and tighter international financing conditions.
… and the macroeconomic environment could quickly worsen in 2027, due to an exceptional El Niño event
The region is particularly vulnerable to climate shocks via the agricultural commodities channel. Given agriculture’s high share in its GDP,
sub-Saharan Africa is more exposed to downside risks to growth than Latin America, Asia or Central Europe (1 st column of the heatmap).

It is also more exposed than other regions of the world to the inflationary effect of rising food prices, given their higher share in the
consumer basket (2 nd column). Finally, even though its dependence on food imports is lower than in Asia and Europe (3 rd column), the
region is highly vulnerable to rising international food commodity prices due to its fragile external accounts. For several countries in our
sample, foreign exchange reserves cover less than four months’ worth of imports of goods and services (Uganda, Tanzania, Ghana and

Zambia), leaving little room for manoeuvre for absorbing a further external shock.
In addition to shocks via the agricultural commodities channel, other localised risks must be considered. In Southern Africa, El Niño is
expected to intensify droughts and threaten electricity generation as a result, particularly in Zambia (where hydropower accounts
for nearly 80% of domestic electricity generation). In East Africa, the increased flooding that El Niño is expected to cause will threaten

infrastructure – particularly logistics infrastructure – which is already less efficient than the global average 3. These two sources of
vulnerability could also increase pressure on growth, prices and the external accounts.
In conclusion, if oil prices remain high and El Niño is exceptionally severe, the region will struggle to absorb this double shock in 2027.
Despite their status as net exporters of hydrocarbons, Nigeria and Angola are also exposed to a deterioration in the macroeconomic

environment in 2027. Only South Africa appears to be better positioned to absorb the shock.
lucas.ple@bnpparibas.com
3 See the World Bank’s Logistics Performance Index.

ToAlpha
2026 年 11 ⽉ 13 ⽇
toalpha.tw
美國｜AI 加速器｜台灣 AI 供應鏈｜純產業研究，⾮投資建議
後⿈仁勳時代的輝達：2027 年出貨⼤致鎖定，供應鏈⾵險移向
2028 年後之產品節奏與 ASIC 分流
執⾏⻑更替對 AMD、博通與 ASIC 陣營、輝達直接供應鏈與 32 家台灣 AI 供應鏈公司之影響（含接班三
情境與份額敏感度）
2027 年以前之出貨與產能已⼤致鎖定，衝擊先落在評價⽽⾮營收
Vera Rubin 8 ⽉開始量產出貨，主要出貨量在 2027 年；輝達對供應商之採
購與產能承諾達 2,790 億美元，CoWoS 約九成由台積電在台灣封裝。新任
董事⻑表⽰客⼾承諾與產品路線圖不會中斷，11 ⽉ 17 ⽇之第三季財報是
第⼀個驗證點。
改變的是新增融資與決策速度：ToAlpha 基本情境輝達之
CoWoS 份額相對原本路徑 2027 年少 2、2028 年少 4 個百分點
管理層 8 ⽉表⽰明年約四分之⼀業務需輝達資產負債表⽀撐；新任管理層

若縮減新增融資，部分需求轉向 AMD 與 ASIC、部分隨之消失。⿈仁勳拍
板之產能預訂與規格升級亦可能放慢，影響最快在 2028 年顯現。CoWoS
總量相對原本路徑僅約 −0.6%、−0.8%。
Google TPU 與 AMD 最直接受益，份額移轉在事件前已開始
據供應鏈訪查，輝達占 CoWoS 需求由 2026 年 56% 降⾄ 2027 年 45%，
AMD 升⾄ 20%；博通預期 AI 營收 2027 會計年度約 1,150 億美元。10 ⽉
28 ⽇ ⾄  11 ⽉  11 ⽇ ， 費 城 半 導 體 指 數  −9.6% ， 輝 達  −19.3% ， AMD
−4.4%、博通 −1.9%。
份額不會流向中國；韓國之⾓⾊在⿈仁勳任內已擴⼤，美國是⽣
產地點之轉移
HBM 主要在韓國，推論晶⽚交三星代⼯、韓國封裝擴產均為⿈仁勳任內之
決定；美國擴產主要由台積電、鴻海、緯創執⾏。2027 年以前，真正之移
轉發⽣在台灣供應鏈內部，由輝達平台流向 ASIC。
以輝達平台為主之台股：AI 營收相對原本路徑 2027 年少約

3.4%、2028 年少約 6.7%
華碩、技嘉、廣達、和碩、英業達之 AI 營收以輝達 GPU 機種為主，2027
年訂單已排定，影響不⼤；不利處在缺少 ASIC 成⻑動能與 2028 年之份額
移轉。以 ASIC 為主者約增 1.5%、3.6%。10 ⽉ 29 ⽇⾄ 11 ⽉ 12 ⽇，以
ASIC 為 主 之  10 家 平 均  −9.4% ， 以 輝 達 為 主 與 兩 邊 都 有 者  −14.0% 、
−17.0%（加權 −7.1%）：市場只區分出 ASIC。

事件
創辦⼈在董事會調查與國會壓⼒下辭職，財務⻑暫代，正式⼈選由外部顧問協助遴選
兩週內由「董事會⽀持」轉為辭職：先是外國情報⾏動之報導，後是與屬下關係之報導與國會壓⼒
10 ⽉ 29 ⽇（美東時間，下同）媒體報導美國情報評估，⼀名晶⽚買家之主管把資訊傳回北京、並與⿈仁勳關係密切；11
⽉ 4 ⽇眾議院三位委員會主席致函邀其出席閉⾨簡報，11 ⽉ 6 ⽇媒體再報導政府備忘錄認定該⼈為中國情報⼈員。11 ⽉ 8
⽇董事會宣布由獨⽴董事委員會調查，表⽰沒有理由相信國家安全或輝達機密遭到危害、仍信任其領導；⿈仁勳同⽇致信
員⼯承認對婚姻不忠。11 ⽉ 10 ⽇獨⽴董事委任前紐約南區代理聯邦檢察官主持調查；同⽇有媒體報導相關公司網絡協助
⾄少 75 億美元之輝達晶⽚流⼊中國，此數字未經其他媒體證實，輝達先前亦稱相關轉運指控已查明不實。11 ⽉ 11 ⽇兩黨
議員陸續要求其辭職，晚間媒體報導其與⼀位直屬⾼階主管之關係（輝達未回應），董事會當晚開會並接受辭呈，11 ⽉ 12
⽇上午（台北時間 21:52）以 8-K 公告。

治理結構同步調整：⾸度設⽴董事⻑，暫代執⾏⻑兼任財務⻑
⿈仁勳辭去總裁、執⾏⻑與董事，公司發⾔⼈表⽰其⽇後不以任何形式參與輝達。財務⻑ Colette Kress（2013 年加⼊，
曾任 Cisco 與 Microsoft 事業部財務主管）暫代執⾏⻑並續任財務⻑；1993 年起擔任董事、曾任 Synopsys 執⾏⻑之
Harvey C. Jones 出任輝達⾸任董事⻑（輝達過去不設董事⻑）。董事會將考慮內外部⼈選並委請外部公司協助，未公布時
程。第三季財報維持 11 ⽉ 17 ⽇公布；據眾議院外交委員會主席表⽰，⿈仁勳同意於感恩節前赴國會作證。

2027 年以前
2027 年之出貨⼤致鎖定：產品已量產、產能已預付、承諾已簽訂
Vera Rubin 已量產出貨，主要出貨量在 2027 年；Rubin Ultra 以前之規格已定案
輝達 8 ⽉表⽰ Vera Rubin 於當⽉開始量產出貨，管理層預估約占第三季資料中⼼營收兩成；鴻海表⽰ Vera Rubin 第四季
放量、為明年主⼒，廣達表⽰第四季⼩量、2027 年第⼀季放量。管理層 8 ⽉預估 2028 會計年度（約當 2027 年 2 ⽉⾄
2028 年 1 ⽉）營收成⻑約七成，屬供給受限之數字；3 ⽉ GTC 時稱 Blackwell 與 Vera Rubin ⾄ 2027 年之訂單能⾒度⾄少

1 兆美元。據供應鏈訪查，Rubin Ultra 與 Feynman 之開發已進⼊執⾏階段；新任管理層第⼀個能實質調整的，是
Feynman 世代之規格、代⼯與封裝分配。
供應商受合約保護：2,790 億美元之採購與產能承諾，加上客⼾之預付款
輝達 7 ⽉底之季報揭露表外之供應與產能採購承諾 2,790 億美元（前⼀季 1,190 億美元），主要為記憶體與製造設施，台積
電、HBM 廠與主要組裝廠 2027 年之產能多已被預訂。下游⽅⾯，廣達 11 ⽉ 11 ⽇法說會表⽰，迄今未⾒客⼾調整訂單，
客⼾若延後拉貨需負擔相關利息與費⽤；以 CoreWeave 為例，其積壓訂單約 1,325 億美元，未來三個⽉另有約 90 億美元
之客⼾預付款與押⾦，綁定的是機房與晶⽚。
近期之⼲擾來⾃轉換期與出⼝管制，⽽⾮需求
據供應鏈訪查，第四季 VR200、Trainium 3 與 TPU v8 之新平台轉換均因零組件與組裝良率⽽遞延，屬時程問題。較需留
意的是出⼝管制：據報美國商務部正敲定新規，把⾺來西亞、新加坡等 7 國納⼊較嚴格之限制，涵蓋 Blackwell 以上晶⽚

及遠端使⽤；和碩 11 ⽉ 12 ⽇法說會表⽰此類限制並⾮新規，已納⼊法務審核。國會亦把晶⽚追蹤（Chip Security Act）
與出⼝許可審查（AI Overwatch Act）列為國防授權法之候選條⽂。本次事件涉及外國情報與晶⽚流向之指控，調查結果
出爐前，管制收緊之機率已上升，台灣組裝廠出貨東南亞機房之合規成本將提⾼。
接班情境
三種接班路徑對產品節奏、供應商議價與客⼾融資之影響不同；ToAlpha 以內部技術主
管接任為基本情境
董事會之⾸要任務是穩定客⼾與⼯程團隊，內部⼈接任之機率最⾼
據報導與預測市場，內部⼈選以 CUDA 創始⼈、現負責⼤型雲端與 HPC 業務之 Ian Buck 呼聲最⾼，其次為暫代之
Kress；技術⻑ Michael Kagan、⾸席科學家 Bill Dally 與 GPU ⼯程主管 Jonah Alben 亦被提及。外部⼈選包括曾任職輝
3 / 12

### Page 4

達七年之 Arm 執⾏⻑ Rene Haas；Apple 硬體主管 Johny Srouji 據報亦在考慮之列，美國總統則據報公開推薦前 Apple
執⾏⻑ Tim Cook。全球業務主管 Jay Puri 已於 6 ⽉宣布退休。輝達沒有公開之接班計畫、約 60 位主管直接向⿈仁勳報
告；正式⼈選產⽣前由 Kress 暫代，市場預期約⼀年。ToAlpha 以內部技術主管接任為基本情境（45%），營運財務型（含
Kress 續任或外部營運型）30%，外部產業執⾏⻑ 25%。
無論何⼈接任，新增融資與規格升級之積極程度都可能低於⿈仁勳時代
輝達 7 ⽉底持有⾮上市股權約 512 億美元、上市股權約 428 億美元，另有最⾼ 1,085 億美元之保證與 250 億美元之股權投
資承諾；運算承諾⽅⾯，OpenAI 規劃約 12GW 之輝達運算、Anthropic 最多 1GW。已簽承諾會延續，但新增部分之審核
標準可能提⾼：據報 Kress 曾反對為 OpenAI 提供最⾼ 1,050 億美元之擔保；市場亦期待新任管理層提⾼現⾦回饋、回應

「循環融資」之疑慮。規格升級⽅⾯，據供應鏈訪查，⿈仁勳之產品策略對供應鏈有「產能預訂」與「規格升級」兩項激
進影響，我們認為這兩項也是輝達供應鏈過去享有較⾼評價之重要原因。新任管理層若偏重成本與執⾏穩定，800V
HVDC、共同封裝光學、M9 等級銅箔基板等升級之導⼊可能放慢，供應鏈成本管理亦會更嚴格。

資料來源：ToAlpha。⼈選為媒體報導與預測市場所列，⾮公司公布；機率為 ToAlpha 主觀判斷，將隨董事會遴選進度調整。
產品路線
Feynman 以前之路線圖已進⼊執⾏，新任管理層可調整之處在決策速度與規格升級
路線圖本⾝未變，但 2026 年已出現取捨：Rubin CPX 擱置、Kyber 機櫃傳延⾄ 2028 年
Rubin 為兩顆光罩極限尺⼨晶粒、每顆搭載 288GB HBM4 之設計[5]，量產爬坡已受 HBM4 規格與良率牽制。輝達今年並
把原訂 2026 年底之 Rubin CPX 移出路線圖，改以 Groq 授權技術之 LPX 推論機櫃處理解碼；產業研究機構 7 ⽉指出
Kyber 機櫃因 PCB 中背板之可製造性延⾄ 2028 年，輝達回應路線圖不變。這顯⽰即使在⿈仁勳任內，年更節奏已觸及供

應鏈之物理極限。我們認為新任管理層較可能之調整，是把年更改為「⼤世代兩年、中間做強化版」，並集中資源於推論
（LPX、Vera CPU）與網路（Spectrum-X、共同封裝光學）。
開放策略可能加速：NVLink Fusion 讓 ASIC 客⼾也採購輝達元件
NVLink Fusion 已納⼊ AWS Trainium4、Marvell 客製 XPU 與 Intel 客製 x86 CPU，輝達 3 ⽉並⼊股 Marvell 20 億美元。
對新任管理層⽽⾔，與其與 ASIC 正⾯競爭，不如在 ASIC 機櫃內銷售 CPU、網卡、交換器與 NVLink；輝達之營收來源因
此分散，但 GPU 本⾝之份額讓渡也會更快。對台灣供應鏈⽽⾔，交換器、網卡相關供應商不必選邊。

AMD∕博通∕ASIC
Google TPU 與 AMD 最直接受益，CoWoS 份額之移轉在事件前已開始
事件前之路徑：輝達占 CoWoS 需求由 2026 年 56% 降⾄ 2027 年 45%
據供應鏈訪查，2027 年全球 CoWoS 需求約 2,694 千⽚，輝達 1,222 千⽚、成⻑ 57%，⾮輝達合計成⻑ 140%；AMD 因
OpenAI、Meta 與 Anthropic 之 MI450 系列放量，份額由 9% 升⾄ 20%，聯發科（Google TPU）由 3% 升⾄ 7%。產業研
究機構亦預期 TPU 出貨 2027 年倍增以上，⾼於輝達⾼階 GPU 約三成之成⻑。換⾔之，輝達之份額下滑早於醜聞；接班不

確定性讓既有趨勢加速，⽽⾮創造新趨勢。
Google TPU 最直接：客⼾與供應鏈之信任轉移最快
據供應鏈訪查，在輝達⾯臨接班不確定性下，Google TPU 最可能先取得客⼾與供應鏈之進⼀步信任，設計夥伴博通與聯發
科最直接受惠。博通 9 ⽉表⽰ AI 營收 2026 會計年度約 580 億美元、2027 年約 1,150 億、2028 年約 2,300 億美元，六家
XPU 客⼾中 Anthropic 2027 年部署 5GW、2028 年再 10GW，OpenAI ⾃研推論晶⽚ 2027 年 1.3GW、2028 年逾 5GW。聯
發科表⽰第⼆顆 ASIC 按計畫於 2028 年量產。
AMD：具名承諾最多 14GW，2027 年起才⼤量部署，能否接住移轉取決於 Helios 機櫃之量產速度
AMD 第三季資料中⼼營收 85.5 億美元、年增 97%，MI455X 已開始量產出貨，具名承諾 OpenAI 6GW、Meta 6GW、
Anthropic 2GW。據供應鏈訪查，Helios 機櫃之⼤量出貨在 2027 年第⼆季。AMD 之 MI350 把 3 奈⽶運算晶粒 3D 堆疊於

6 奈⽶ I/O 晶粒之上[4]，MI450 系列據報再採 2 奈⽶運算晶粒、每顆 432GB HBM4，以單晶⽚規格⾒⻑，但機櫃級整合之
經驗仍少於輝達。輝達若在 2027 年邊際讓出份額，AMD 之瓶頸是 CoWoS 與 HBM 配額，⽽⾮客⼾意願。
⾦額份額與顆數份額差距⼤：ASIC 顆數 2028 年超越 GPU，⾦額仍以輝達為主
產業研究機構估計客製 ASIC 出貨顆數將於 2028 年超越資料中⼼ GPU；以⾦額計，據供應鏈訪查 ASIC 占加速器市場由約
⼀成升⾄ 2027 年約兩成。輝達 Vera Rubin 每 GW 營收約 400 億美元，博通估其每 GW 內容價值約 250 億美元（其估計之
中值）。份額移轉對輝達營收之影響因此⼩於對出貨顆數之影響；但對依顆數與機櫃數計價之台灣組裝與零組件供應商，影
響較接近顆數。
技術差距縮⼩：AMD 與 ASIC 之單晶⽚規格追近，輝達之優勢轉向機櫃整合與軟體
以廠商公布之稠密峰值計，AMD MI455X 之 FP4 約 40 PFLOPS、Rubin 約 35 PFLOPS[2]（輝達官⽅標⽰之 50 PFLOPS 為

NVFP4 推論值）；Google TPU 歷經五代維持架構穩定，以規模與能效取勝[1]。輝達之差異化愈來愈落在 NVLink 機櫃整
合、網路與 CUDA ⽣態，⽽這些正是需要領導⼈跨部⾨拍板之領域；接班空窗期之⾵險，在於競爭者單晶⽚規格追近之
際，輝達之系統整合節奏放慢。

### Page 6

ToAlpha 份額移轉之推導：融資收斂與客⼾分散下單，2027 年約 2、2028 年約 4 個百分點
2027 年：明年約四分之⼀業務需輝達融資⽀撐，若新增融資縮減⼀⾄⼆成，約為輝達量 2.5%〜5%，以 45% 份額換算約
1〜2 個百分點，加上客⼾邊際分散下單，取 2 個百分點（1.5〜2.5）。2028 年：融資依賴之需求縮減約兩成（約輝達量
5%），加上⼤型客⼾新專案約 4% 轉向 AMD 與 ASIC，合計約輝達量 9%、約 4 個百分點（3〜5）。輝達減少之量，2027 年
七成、2028 年⼋成由 AMD 與 ASIC 依原份額吸收，其餘為依賴輝達融資、隨之消失之需求，CoWoS 總量因此少約 0.6%、
0.8%。供應商營收以平台出貨量⽐例估算，未區分每⽚晶圓之價值差異。

之⽐重為 ToAlpha 依法說會說法取整之假設，個別公司可能與組別平均差異明顯；台積電與⽇⽉光投控以 CoWoS 總量計。影響為 AI 營收，⾮淨利。
韓國∕中國∕美國
台灣份額 2027 年前難以外移；韓國之⾓⾊在⿈仁勳任內已擴⼤，美國是⽣產地點之轉
移，中國沒有承接能⼒
物理限制：輝達之 CoWoS 約九成在台灣封裝，美國之 CoWoS 級產能最早 2028 年初
據供應鏈訪查，輝達 2027 年約 1,222 千⽚之 CoWoS 需求中，約 132 千⽚由 Amkor 承接，其餘由台積電封裝。美國⽅

⾯，Amkor 亞利桑那廠 2028 年初量產、台積電亞利桑那封裝廠尚無 CoWoS 量產時程、SK 海⼒⼠印第安那 HBM 封裝廠
6 / 12

### Page 7

2029 年、三星泰勒⼆廠 2030 年；亞利桑那⽣產之 Blackwell 晶圓⽬前仍須運回台灣封裝。因此 2027 年以前之 Rubin 與
Rubin Ultra，前段晶圓與封裝⾼度依賴台灣；新任管理層第⼀個可調整之世代是 Feynman。
韓國：HBM 主要在韓國，增量在封裝與代⼯，且多為⿈仁勳任內之決定
據業界估計，Rubin 之 HBM4 由 SK 海⼒⼠供應約三分之⼆、三星約四分之⼀以上；產業研究機構 6 ⽉⼤幅上修三星之
HBM4 份額，三星今年亦發表單堆疊 36GB、3.3TB/s 之 HBM4[3]。台灣在 HBM 之⾓⾊主要為美光在台產能。韓國之增量
有兩處：推論晶⽚ LP30 交由三星 4 奈⽶；據報輝達 10 ⽉宣布與 Amkor ⾄ 2028 年底把韓國中介層產能提⾼ 300% 以上。
另據報輝達要求記憶體廠把客製 HBM 之 base die 交台積電代⼯，則對台灣有利。新任管理層若偏重成本與供應分散，韓
國在⾮旗艦產品之代⼯與封裝份額可能再提⾼，但旗艦 GPU 之前段 2028 年前仍無替代。

中國：管制只會收緊，台廠之⾵險在出貨中國客⼾與東南亞機房
輝達財測沿⽤不計⼊中國資料中⼼運算營收之做法；產業研究機構估計 2026 年上半年輝達在中國 AI 加速器之份額降⾄
34%、華為升⾄ 28%。本次事件涉及晶⽚流向中國之指控，新任管理層之合規只會更嚴，供應鏈不會流向中國；受影響的
是出貨中國客⼾或東南亞機房之台灣組裝廠，例如英業達 5 ⽉法說會表⽰其 AI 伺服器以中國客⼾占⽐偏⾼。
美國：地點轉移由台廠執⾏，營收仍在台灣公司，但成本較⾼
美國商務部⻑表⽰⽬標把台灣約四成半導體產能移⾄美國；輝達宣⽰在美國⽣產最多 5,000 億美元之 AI 基礎設施，緯創德
州廠已⽣產 GB300 並準備 Vera Rubin，鴻海休⼠頓廠興建中、規劃⽣產 GB300 運算模組。新任執⾏⻑在國安疑慮之政治
環境下，加速「美國製造」之誘因更⾼；但承接者仍是台積電、鴻海、緯創、廣達等台廠，屬地點轉移。美國本⼟之替代
以 Intel 與 Amkor 為主：據報 Feynman 之 I/O 晶粒與最多四分之⼀之最終封裝可能交 Intel，據供應鏈訪查 Google TPU

後續世代預計採 Intel EMIB-T 封裝；這類分散化之決定權在新任管理層⼿上。

台廠為主 美國廠多在興建 規格升級節奏放慢之影響⼤於份額
資料來源：輝達、台積電、Amkor、SK 海⼒⼠、三星、產業研究機構、據供應鏈訪查；ToAlpha 整理。
直接供應鏈
量之衝擊⼩於規格升級節奏之衝擊：2027 年之價值成⻑相當部分來⾃ Vera Rubin 之規
格
機櫃數之估計分歧，單櫃價值翻倍是共同看法
據供應鏈訪查，NVL72 機櫃出貨 2026 年約 82 千櫃、2027 年約 90 千櫃；產業研究機構則估計 2027 年成⻑逾五成，兩者
差距⼤。共同之看法是 Vera Rubin 系統平均售價約為 GB300 之兩倍，廣達亦表⽰新世代 GPU 伺服器單價⾼四到五成。
7 / 12

### Page 8

2027 年輝達供應鏈之成⻑相當部分來⾃單櫃價值，⽽單櫃價值來⾃規格升級，這正是新任管理層最可能放慢之部分。組裝
份額⽅⾯，鴻海由 2026 年約 37% 升⾄ 2027 年約 43%，廣達約 26%，緯創約 24%。
受影響之先後：規格升級受惠者最先，依機櫃數計價者次之，晶圓與封裝最後
800V HVDC 電源（台達電、光寶科）、共同封裝光學、M9 等級銅箔基板（台光電、聯茂）、液冷與雙⾯冷板（奇鋐、雙鴻）
等受惠於規格升級之供應商，其時點已隨 Kyber 傳延⾄ 2028 年；更替使「升級是否如期」之不確定性增加。依機櫃數計
價之組裝、滑軌、機構件次之。台積電與 CoWoS 因產能不⾜、可轉給 AMD 與 ASIC，受影響最⼩。另據供應鏈訪查，台灣
供應商將⾯臨更嚴格之價格談判，訂單亦可能進⼀步分散；新任管理層若以成本管理⾒⻑，台灣組裝廠本已偏薄之⽑利率
將承壓。
終端客⼾結構亦重要：neocloud 對輝達融資之依賴⾼於⼤型雲端業者
⼤型雲端業者以⾃有現⾦流與⻑約採購，neocloud 與前沿實驗室則較依賴輝達之⼊股、保底合約與擔保。若新任管理層收

緊新增融資，受影響之需求集中在 neocloud；和碩表⽰其伺服器⽬前以 neocloud 與⽩牌為主。同樣以輝達平台為主，客
⼾以⼤型雲端業者為主者（如廣達）較為穩定。
台股曝險
以輝達平台為主之台股：2027 年影響有限，不利處在缺少 ASIC 動能與 2028 年之份額
移轉
市場⽬前只區分出 ASIC：以 ASIC 為主者相對抗跌，純輝達與兩邊都有者差異不⼤
10 ⽉ 29 ⽇⾄ 11 ⽉ 12 ⽇，加權指數 −7.1%，32 家中以 ASIC 為主之 10 家平均 −9.4%、以輝達平台為主之 5 家 −14.0%、
兩邊都有之 17 家 −17.0%；11 ⽉ 12 ⽇傳出董事會準備撤換之當⽇，三組分別 −5.2%、−5.3%、−2.8%（加權 −2.6%）。此
期間同時有美國期中選舉後之科技股修正，兩邊都有之組別多為前波漲幅⼤之零組件股，跌幅亦⼤，個股差異不全來⾃本
事件。市場對純輝達者尚未額外折價，反映 2027 年之訂單能⾒度。
純輝達之台股：2027 年營收有保護，不利處在缺少 ASIC 動能與 2028 年之份額移轉

以輝達平台為主之五家，2027 年之 Vera Rubin 出貨已排定，單價提⾼四到五成，營收成⻑仍可期；ToAlpha 基本情境之
份額移轉僅使其 2027 年 AI 營收相對原本路徑少約 3.4%。不利之處有三：其⼀，缺少 2027〜2028 年成⻑最快之 ASIC 動
能；其⼆，2028 年份額移轉使其 AI 營收少約 6.7%，若規格升級放慢，單櫃價值之成⻑亦打折；其三，依賴輝達之配貨與
融資，新任管理層之成本與融資政策直接影響其⽑利與客⼾。廣達已表⽰⾮ GPU 平台 2027 年將升⾄雙位數、和碩有 4〜5
家 ASIC 客⼾將於 2027 年放量，組裝廠可逐步降低集中度；我們認為華碩、技嘉之伺服器業務較難轉向 ASIC，因雲端業者
⾃研晶⽚多直接委託 ODM。華碩、技嘉、和碩之⾮ AI 業務占⽐亦⾼，股價受 AI 以外因素影響⼤。緯創⺟公司以輝達運算
板為主，惟合併⼦公司緯穎後屬兩邊都有。
兩邊都有之供應商：份額移轉之影響約 −1%，⾵險在規格升級節奏
奇鋐表⽰「GPU 與 ASIC ⾮⼆選⼀、兩邊都出貨」；據供應鏈訪查，雙鴻之 ASIC 營收 2027 年可望約占四成。這類供應商在

份額移轉下之 AI 營收相對原本路徑少約 1.3%（2027 年）、2.3%（2028 年），其⾵險在規格升級之節奏，⽽⾮平台選擇。
台積電與⽇⽉光投控依 CoWoS 總量計，影響約 −0.6%、−0.8%。

服器出貨之實際影響。
客⼾之分散下單：⼤型雲端業者與 neocloud 是否把邊際訂單轉向 AMD 與 ASIC，以及 AMD Helios 之量產速度。
⾵險
本報告之主要不確定性在於接班⼈選、調查結果與 CoWoS 產能是否仍不⾜
接班⼈選與調查結果：董事會調查與國會調查若發現更多出⼝管制問題，影響可能擴及輝達之營運許可，份額移轉將超
出本報告區間；反之若迅速確定具技術背景之接班⼈，2028 年之移轉可能⼩於基本情境。
產能與吸收假設：本報告假設 CoWoS 與 HBM 2027〜2028 年仍不⾜、輝達減少之量七⾄⼋成由 AMD 與 ASIC 吸收。若
產能轉為過剩或依賴融資之需求消失更多，份額移轉將伴隨總量下降，對台積電與兩邊都有之供應商亦不利。
分組與⽐重：各公司之輝達⽐重為依法說會說法之 ToAlpha 判斷，部分公司未揭露客⼾結構；供應商營收以平台出貨量

⽐例估算，未區分每⽚晶圓之價值。個別公司之實際影響可能與所屬分組平均有明顯差異。
股價區間：事件期間之股價差異同時受美國期中選舉後之科技股修正與個別公司消息影響，不宜全數歸因於本事件。

重要聲明
本報告由 ToAlpha（toalpha.tw）研究部編製，屬純產業研究，⾮投資建議。本報告僅描述產業、供應鏈與情境影響，不對任何個股提供評等、⽬標
價或買進、賣出、持有之建議，亦不構成任何證券之要約或要約之引誘。報告中之份額變動、營收影響與情境機率係 ToAlpha 依公開資料、供應鏈訪
查與⾃訂假設推算，涉及多項假設，實際結果可能與本報告所述有重⼤差異。本報告所引⽤之資料雖來⾃我們認為可靠之來源，惟 ToAlpha 不保證其
正確性與完整性，資料與觀點如有變動恕不另⾏通知。讀者應⾃⾏判斷並承擔投資⾵險，ToAlpha 對任何⼈依本報告所為之決定不負任何責任。未經

### Page 1

The Goldman Sachs Group, Inc.
Global bond yields have risen to multi-decade highs, yet growth and markets have
remained remarkably resilient. But with some pressures beginning to emerge (e.g. in
European sovereign bond markets), can this resilience last, or will more vulnerabilities
be revealed? We ask the IMF’s Tobias Adrian, Moody’s Analytics’ Mark Zandi, and GS
economists. Adrian is relatively sanguine about the economy’s resilience, though
notes downside risks, while GS economists expect only a modest growth drag from

higher rates, but a more consequential one if yields remain near current or higher
levels. Zandi is generally more concerned, describing the US economy as vulnerable
rather than resilient, and sees one-in-three odds of the economy going off the r ails.
Meanwhile, GS strategists and analysts are not especially concerned about banks or
markets more broadly, but warn that further increases in yields or rate volatility could
test risk assets. Key to watch: whether the increasingly interconnected and leveraged

AI investment boom can continue to withstand higher rates.
Tobias Adrian, Special Advisor and former Director of Monetary and Capital Markets, IMF
“The global economy has demonstrated considerable resilience in the face of multiple severe shocks, and I would not be
surprised if it continues to shrug off higher yields, pr ovided they do not keep rising sharply in a disorderly fashion. At the same
time, I do worry about downside risks.”
Mark Zandi, Chief Economist, Moody’s Analytics

• Positive growth spillovers from the global AI capex boom, 
which we think should continue into 2027.  
• UK growth, which we expect to slow in the next two quarters. 
• French elections; frontrunner Le Pen’s proposal to address 
fiscal challenges is likely to face implementation challenges. 
• Italy’s 2027/2028 fiscal deficit, which was revised up 
significantly, weakening the country’s debt outlook. 
• Rising sovereign stress, which has tightened EA financial 
conditions, reducing the urgency for the ECB to hike rates 
beyond Dec, when we expect the last hike.

We provide a brief snapshot on the most important economies for the global markets

### Page 3

El 
Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research 3 
Top of Mind Issue 152

Recent acceleration in the rise in global bond yields to multi-
decade highs has begun to raise questions about the 
implications for growth and markets. Both have remained 
remarkably resilient so far, with US growth in particular 
holding relatively firm and the S&P 500 and other equity 
markets near new all-time highs. But with pressure 
beginning to emerge—most notably in European sovereign 
bond markets—and yields looking unlikely to retreat soon, 
can that resilience last, or will more vulnerabilities be revealed? 
GS Head of US Rates Strategy William Marshall first lays out 
how we arrived at higher rates, underscoring the striking 
breadth of the rise, which he attributes mainly to 
fundamentals—strong growth, inflation risks, hawkish policy 
shifts, the ongoing investment cycle, and long-run fiscal 
risks—but notes that technical factors likely amplified the 
recent move. And while he ultimately expects yields to fall 
(with YE 10-year US Treasury yields of 4.75%/4.4% in 
2026/2027, respectively) as markets become more confident 
in easing inflation, he thinks elevated long-term yields should 
have some staying power, and sees continued upside risk 
from energy price and fiscal pressures.  
So, just how much of a threat do higher rates pose to the 
macro outlook? For answers, we first turn to Tobias Adrian, 
Special Advisor to the Managing Director and former 
Financial Counsellor and Director of the Monetary and Capital 
Markets Department at the IMF, Mark Zandi, Chief 
Economist of Moody’s Analytics, and GS US economists. 
Adrian sees a global economy that has so far responded to 
the yield shock much as it has to the many other major 
shocks of the past seven years: resiliently. He notes that the 
rise in yields has not significantly tightened financial 
conditions or, more importantly, disrupted market functioning 
or forced damaging deleveraging. He believes that resilience 
could persist as long as the move in yields doesn’t accelerate 
or become more disorderly. But he is concerned about 
vulnerabilities in the AI and technology complex, sovereign 
bond markets, and EMs—not to mention cyber risks—all of 
which leaves him “vigilant but cautiously optimistic.” 
GS US economists also characterize the US economy as 
resilient. Their expectation that the Fed will deliver just one 
more rate hike this year before cutting next year—consistent 
with Marshall’s expectation of lower Treasury yields—leads 
Pierfrancesco Mei to expect only a modest 0.2pp growth 
drag in 2027 from higher rates. But he finds that the drag 
could rise to a more consequential 0.5pp if yields remain near 
current levels (see pg. 9 for GS global economists Joseph 
Briggs’ and Johan Allen’s rules of thumb for the impact of 
higher yields on rate-sensitive areas across major DMs.) 
David Mericle and Mei also worry about the implications for 
the already-concerning US fiscal trajectory, which they 
believe is unlikely to trigger a classic debt crisis but is too risky 
to ignore.     
Zandi, for his part, is generally more concerned. While his 
baseline outlook is relatively sanguine, he describes the US 
economy as vulnerable rather than resilient and views the 
“distribution of possible outcomes as very flat and heavily 
skewed toward darker tail scenarios.” He sees the clearest 
softness in the labor market, with weak hiring and falling real 
wage growth for many workers weighing on consumer 
spending. And he takes little comfort from US growth 
continuing to run around 2%—roughly its potential rate—
noting that AI accounts for roughly one-third of that growth, 
leaving the non-AI economy growing at only around 1-1.5%, 
which he calls “a pretty punk economy.” Zandi also worries 
that the US fiscal situation is “as bad as I have ever seen it” 
and that higher rates are already inflicting damage on real 
estate markets and squeezing existing debtors. As these 
stresses mount, he sees elevated risk of something breaking 
in the financial system. All told, he puts the odds of the 
current vulnerabilities pushing the economy completely off 
the rails at around one-in-three.  
With banks historically at the center of the rate shocks and 
economic crises that Zandi is worried about, we then explore 
how much risk banks pose today. Adrian and GS Head of the 
Financials Equity Group Richard Ramsden both see little 
cause for alarm, explaining that higher rates should boost 
banks’ net interest income and revenues given their asset-
sensitive balance sheets. Ramsden also doesn’t expect a 
significant deterioration in credit quality absent a broader 
economic downturn and says that most banks should have 
excess capital even after accounting for rate-driven portfolio 
losses.   
But even if banks can weather higher rates, what about 
markets more broadly? GS strategists and analysts are not 
especially concerned, with Chief US Equity Strategist Ben 
Snider arguing that current rate levels shouldn’t prevent 
continued strong US earnings growth and the S&P 500 from 
climbing higher, Chief Credit Strategist Amanda Lynam 
viewing the headwind to corporate credit from higher rates 
as manageable amid the solid growth backdrop, and US 
Financials Analyst Alexander Blostein expecting the recent 
slowdown in private market activity to rebound once rates 
stabilize. GS multi-asset strategists Christian Mueller-
Glissmann and Andrea Ferrario add that higher yields have 
strengthened the strategic case for bonds in a multi-asset 
portfolio, though strong earnings continue to favor equities 
for now. But they all warn that if rate volatility remains 
elevated, and especially if yields continue to rise from here, 
risk assets could face mounting pressures.  
All that said, with the AI boom powering the economy and 
markets, everything may come down to whether higher rates 
will derail it. Snider views this as unlikely given the strong 
balance sheets of the firms investing in AI and the expected 
profitability of these investments, with Lynam also seeing a 
high bar to derail AI-related debt issuance. Adrian also 
maintains a positive baseline for the AI economy but is 
concerned about the circularity of investment, increased 
leverage, and the sustainability of revenue growth in the AI 
complex. And Zandi again seems the most worried, warning 
that “AI could become the part of the economy that breaks” 
if rates remain at current levels for much longer.  
Allison Nathan, Editor

Higher bond yields: how concerning?

### Page 4
